Canonical outcomes: brief §4b (all fair results and accepted alternatives) — plan doc: a new Google Doc owned by Claire with the calls by surface type, ship route and dates, reuse and owners; tracker: a new spreadsheet owned by Claire with exactly the 62 serving surfaces (4 store, 56 Unity, ART-826, ART-831; no Archive row), calls Halloween version 14 (ads 12 plus the icon and iPad set), Refresh first 36, Reuse 2, Skip 10; due Fri Oct 10 on the 30 working ads on Android US phone (an earlier October final date accepted), Wed Oct 15 on the other 18 (Oct 11 to Oct 14 accepted), blank on Skip, the icon and iPad set by the Thu Oct 16 12:00 PT merge cut; makers Martin Walsh (icon, store art), Julia Lawson (6 ad statics), Marcus Bennett (42 video and end cards); both files shared with exactly the six review attendees of evt_d947bf7cd910549b674b1f as commenter or writer; one comment on each of the 58 running tickets; the 48 working tickets assigned to their makers; new ART tickets for the icon and the iPad 12.9" set (two tickets, or one naming both); one reply under C06DJBJL798/1759269913.882683 holding both links.

Recorded decisions:

- Due-date window, a recorded difference from brief §4b: the brief allows an earlier internal date from Oct 6 on the Android US phone tier; the due-date rows accept an earlier October 2025 final date for the Oct 10 tier and Oct 11 to Oct 14 for the Oct 15 tier. 'Final' is the operative word: a refresh milestone does not stand in for the date the finished ad is due.
- Comment rows stay, against the local /rubric run 1 findings A1 and A2 (both `duplicate` overlap groups at Moderate, rubric_gen/review2/3_rubric_local_r1_FAIL2.md): the 58 call rows and 48 due-date rows are exhaustive direct coverage of 'Comment each ad's call and due date on its ART ticket', not samples. Reason: the platform ruled this way in support/completed_tasks/frederick_q4_commitments_2026-11-05/eval_reports/3_rubric_FAIL_r1.md (quoting rubric_flow_v2: 'Comments whose content differs per target get one row each, with no cap') and in support/completed_tasks/julia_zm_difficulty_baseline_2026-11-01/eval_reports/3_rubric_FAIL_r5.md ('template sampling is allowed, not required, and each row embeds its own comment's content'), and Evals/references/rules/rubric_sampling.md says there is 'no automatic row-count threshold or requirement to shrink an otherwise valid exhaustive rubric'. The local bundle's eval release differs from the platform's (local run c5b89f4c, this task's platform reports 81446b85), and the two rule sentences those rulings quote are not in the local rubric_flow_v2.md. Local /rubric run 2 accepted the dismissal on all 106 rows, on local rules alone (the Local Override on spot checks, rubric_sampling, QC Appendix C2 on write actions); it gave the platform reports no weight. Run 4 applied the same ruling to the 48 assignment rows. Limits of that evidence: the rulings covered 31 written answers and 7 + 7 comment rows, not 106, and rubric_sampling's sentence follows its five-sample limit, so the case rests on these rows being direct coverage rather than samples.
- Reuse sets: the iPhone 6.5" and Google Play phone sets have no ship-date rows (their rows are P4, P5, P27, P28), following local /rubric run 1 finding A26 (both sets are Reuse, and a plan that says so meets every ask) and its rejected finding X1 (Leonard's 'what we already have, and who makes what by when' and 'whatever is already exported is done': a finished set has no maker, so no by-when is owed).
- Folder note, not a finding: P1 says 'in Claire's Drive', the prompt's own 'in my Drive'. Brief §4b also lists Leonard's folder d_272d849cde40c35980a433, but the prompt does not authorize a folder of his, so the wording rejects nothing the prompt allows; no baseline filed either file there.

Rubric shape: every write family has a result row. The 58 ticket comments differ per target, so 58 call rows and 48 due-date rows take them one per ticket, with no sampling there. The sampled populations (G0 doc counts, G1 tracker calls, G2 tracker due dates, G3 tracker makers) have volume rows and direct rows on the members an authority singles out (gate 15), never a bundled exact-set row. 09_Rubrics.json mirrors P1–P217 one to one (C-n = P-n).

| P216, P217 | "reply in Leonard's thread with both links before the review" | An Outcome does prove it: the reply must hold both links, and a link exists only once its file does; 'before the review' is a deadline the clock meets, since a run happens on Oct 1 and the review is Thu Oct 2 (evt_d947bf7cd910549b674b1f) | fails as a Process row — the prompt orders no other write around the reply, so a valid run may comment or assign after it; the reply only needs links that exist anyway | fails — P216, P217 already reject a reply without either link, the only ordering the prompt states | fails — a timestamp comparison would grade the trace, with no behaviour left to observe | no Process row: P216, P217 carry it as Outcomes with F1; the prompt states no ordering dependency between writes |

## Self-review

Re-read as /rubric: walked Evals/3_Rubrics_Eval.md 1.4 and 3.1 against the plan in order. Every prompt requirement (ledger R1–R12) and every write family (plan doc; tracker; shares; ticket comments; assignments; new tickets; thread reply) has a result row on the correct artifact, and every exclusion sits as a count or scope on a required write (P57, P164, P215). The prompt asks to be told nothing beyond the deliverables, so no row grades the final response; a concise final reply passes the whole set (§A3). Friction is none, so no recovery outcome is needed.

Count on the four bases: 0. The six rev3 baselines read some deciding records rarely (Frederick's Oct 10 reply 0/6; Martin's vacation post 0/6; Marcus's note 0/6; Frederick's Trello comment on end card 001 0/6; Robert's rejection 2/6; baseline_writes.json reads). Those are model misses on records a run could open, as the OE path shows, not an impossible target.

Shortcut and diversity: the §F probe's verdict is HITS (366), with 0 INCOMPLETE lines and 362 SHORTCUT-OK lines, one per record (366 HIT lines, since the Unity tab hits 4 populations). Strongest collapse cases, each read: (1) the sheet's Unity tab holds 56 of 58 ad keys and so most of every population, and a run could list the ads from it, but its columns are Creative, Format, Live since, ART ticket, so no call, due date, maker or campaign sits there, and two running ads are not on it. (2) Frederick's reply C0880U9751A/1759275738.984915 gives the Oct 10 date, but a run still needs each ticket's campaigns to know which ads it binds; finding it is the designed work. (3) The deck's calls slide holds the four call words and no ticket. (4) Each of the 31 ticket comments holds its own key and campaign names only. No hit holds an ad key next to a call, date or maker (checked here over all 32 hits with a key). The other 294 base hits are Gmail and sheet records that hold staff addresses, which are directory facts. Largest unit of discovery: response f_02b1968f5e882366fbb883, 709 of 755 (94%), then response claire's inbox, from:frederick.stone@harmonygames.co, subject 'october ua plan - changes' 37% and response C08R8CHB0QL/1758817822.269981 32%. The live creatives sheet leads because the ledger's locator edges run every ticket fact through it (each ticket F-row has origin F4, the Unity tab); see Unresolved.

Surface dispositions: none; all 34 slots have a covering row, so §G carries no disposition. Strongest case for a further row: 24 slots (S05, S06, S08, S09, S28, S29, S10, S11, S14, S15, S16, S17, S18, S30, S38, S39, S32, S33, S34, S35, S36, S21, S22, S23) name a tracker cell, and rows.json covers them through the ticket comment or the assignment on the same ad, while the tracker's columns are sampled. The value is one fact graded on the write the prompt requires per ticket, and a second row per ad would double the fact without new coverage. Gate 15 keeps each tracker group at five samples or fewer, and §G says so on each such line.

Recorded decisions, re-checked against their sources in this build:

- Due-date window, a recorded difference from brief §4b: the brief allows an earlier internal date from Oct 6 on the Android US phone tier; the due-date rows accept an earlier October 2025 final date for the Oct 10 tier and Oct 11 to Oct 14 for the Oct 15 tier. 'Final' is the operative word: a refresh milestone does not stand in for the date the finished ad is due.
- Comment rows stay, against the local /rubric run 1 findings A1 and A2 (both `duplicate` overlap groups at Moderate, rubric_gen/review2/3_rubric_local_r1_FAIL2.md): the 58 call rows and 48 due-date rows are exhaustive direct coverage of 'Comment each ad's call and due date on its ART ticket', not samples. Reason: the platform ruled this way in support/completed_tasks/frederick_q4_commitments_2026-11-05/eval_reports/3_rubric_FAIL_r1.md (quoting rubric_flow_v2: 'Comments whose content differs per target get one row each, with no cap') and in support/completed_tasks/julia_zm_difficulty_baseline_2026-11-01/eval_reports/3_rubric_FAIL_r5.md ('template sampling is allowed, not required, and each row embeds its own comment's content'), and Evals/references/rules/rubric_sampling.md says there is 'no automatic row-count threshold or requirement to shrink an otherwise valid exhaustive rubric'. The local bundle's eval release differs from the platform's (local run c5b89f4c, this task's platform reports 81446b85), and the two rule sentences those rulings quote are not in the local rubric_flow_v2.md. Local /rubric run 2 accepted the dismissal on all 106 rows, on local rules alone (the Local Override on spot checks, rubric_sampling, QC Appendix C2 on write actions); it gave the platform reports no weight. Run 4 applied the same ruling to the 48 assignment rows. Limits of that evidence: the rulings covered 31 written answers and 7 + 7 comment rows, not 106, and rubric_sampling's sentence follows its five-sample limit, so the case rests on these rows being direct coverage rather than samples.
- Reuse sets: the iPhone 6.5" and Google Play phone sets have no ship-date rows (their rows are P4, P5, P27, P28), following local /rubric run 1 finding A26 (both sets are Reuse, and a plan that says so meets every ask) and its rejected finding X1 (Leonard's 'what we already have, and who makes what by when' and 'whatever is already exported is done': a finished set has no maker, so no by-when is owed).
- Folder note, not a finding: P1 says 'in Claire's Drive', the prompt's own 'in my Drive'. Brief §4b also lists Leonard's folder d_272d849cde40c35980a433, but the prompt does not authorize a folder of his, so the wording rejects nothing the prompt allows; no baseline filed either file there.
